**Projected strength total: 73:30 (73.5 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Double-kettlebell clean fallback:** Familiar two-hand swing, same sets of 5 with 90 s rest; otherwise light two-hand KB deadlift, same sets of 3 with fast stand/controlled return. Do not learn a new fallback in W6.
**Supported dumbbell single-leg RDL fallback:** Same single-leg hinge with lighter load/shorter ROM and hand support; no substitution to the excluded bilateral variation
**Prone Y fallback:** Familiar bilateral prone Y/T 2×12, same easy reserve and 60 s rest; same two-arm timing allowance. An unplanned unilateral replacement needs a new clock.
**Landmine rotation fallback:** High-to-low cable chop, identical sets/reps/reserve/rest. Distinct from Wednesday’s low-to-high lift fallback. Avoid forced lumbar twisting; choose the familiar version before reduced weeks.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If recovery binds: added single-leg hinge first, then reduce nonessential remaining work. More available time is for full preparation/recovery, not bonus sets. Protect safe tests/main lifts and shoulder/adductor work; symptoms override any cut label. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


## Week 5

### Week 5 — Sunday

Fresh dip priority; subordinate paused bench; horizontal pulling. Start ≥6 h after the long ride FINISHES.

**Before strength:** no impact. Sunday starts ≥6 hours after actual ride completion; no impact after a ride or fatigued lifting.

**Projected strength total: 67:00 (67 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Single-arm kettlebell clean fallback:** Independent-arm chest-supported machine speed row, same sets, 3/side, ≤50% of that day’s chosen normal row load, fast pull/2 s return; same rest. Upper-body alternative, not an equivalent ballistic stimulus.
**Chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. If no independent-arm station exists Wednesday: use bilateral chest-supported rows at the same set count AND replace its suitcase carry with one-arm overhead KB hold 1×15 s/side, easy, no press reps. This separate upper-limb hold supplies unilateral work and costs ≤the carry allowance; flag the lost carry exposure, a flexible floor.
**Cable external rotation fallback:** Band external rotation 2×12/side, same reserve/rest/side-change allowance.
**Hollow-body hold fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver) → secondary bench. Protect dip and shoulder work. Sunday power must stay easy on the legs after the long ride; regress to the supported upper-body fallback if needed. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If local fatigue or schedule binds: added lunge dose first, then a Pallof set; do not relabel Copenhagen as the additional accessory. Preserve safe pull-up, conventional and second OHP exposure. If protected work still cannot fit the real window, record the missed work and revise rather than rush rests. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


### Week 5 — Wednesday

Fresh heavy OHP priority; pull-ups, rows and protected shoulder/trunk work.

Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

**Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw fallback:** Low-to-high cable lift: same sets/reps per side, lightest smooth setting, fast outward phase/controlled return; same side changes and 90 s rests. This preserves a distinct dynamic pattern but is not a ballistic throw.
**Single-arm chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. **Body saw fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.

**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.

Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.

Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.

## Week 6

### Week 6 — Sunday

Fresh dip priority; subordinate paused bench; horizontal pulling. Start ≥6 h after the long ride FINISHES.

**Before strength:** no impact. Sunday starts ≥6 hours after actual ride completion; no impact after a ride or fatigued lifting.

**Projected strength total: 44:30 (44.5 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Single-arm kettlebell clean fallback:** Independent-arm chest-supported machine speed row, same sets, 3/side, ≤50% of that day’s chosen normal row load, fast pull/2 s return; same rest. Upper-body alternative, not an equivalent ballistic stimulus.
**Chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. If no independent-arm station exists Wednesday: use bilateral chest-supported rows at the same set count AND replace its suitcase carry with one-arm overhead KB hold 1×15 s/side, easy, no press reps. This separate upper-limb hold supplies unilateral work and costs ≤the carry allowance; flag the lost carry exposure, a flexible floor.
**Cable external rotation fallback:** Band external rotation 2×12/side, same reserve/rest/side-change allowance.
**Hollow-body hold fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver) → secondary bench. Protect dip and shoulder work. Sunday power must stay easy on the legs after the long ride; regress to the supported upper-body fallback if needed. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. ### Week 6 — Wednesday

Fresh heavy OHP priority; pull-ups, rows and protected shoulder/trunk work.

**Impact clock: 15 minutes maximum including travel and preparation.** Planned activity at the site: 4:35; full dose leaves 10:25 for travel/extra preparation.

| Order | Activity | Dose / full recovery | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Low bilateral pogos | 2×10; 30 s between sets | 50 |
| 3 | Ordinary transitions / measuring | Marker changes, measurement and movement between drills not already counted | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 20, moderate 0, high 0. **Running:** 0 acceleration + 0 runout = 0 m.

Start the clock before travel. Compare actual transit/setup plus this full prescription with the cap BEFORE work. Cut last run (normally 132 s saved), then final high training jump (100 s), then another run; Wednesday cut the final high training jump or final moderate set as applicable. Preserve full preparation/rest. A test needs all three planned attempts for the best-of-three protocol; if it cannot fit, defer the measurement rather than call a partial test complete. Record actual contacts/meters; reduced work does not qualify a larger stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday.

Progress only after two actually tolerated exposures of the same relevant mode/stage and normal next mornings. Running exposures and jump exposures are separate. Deload technique does not qualify a higher stage; restore only a dose actually tolerated before deload. Outside approved test, first-entry and post-deload restoration exceptions, compare actual weekly contacts by tier with the last completed comparable normal week: keep any increase ≤15% (round down to whole contacts); small high-tier totals usually mean hold. Time/symptom cuts do not authorize a larger automatic next-week dose. Review any proposed upward exception. No faster run stage is added because Friday has more time.

After impact and the following morning record normal/abnormal. If abnormal: location, severity, onset/duration, gait/stride/squat effect and gentle resisted-adduction response. Familiar mild soreness resolving in 24–48 h without movement change: hold, do not progress. Increasing/persistent symptoms or reduced output: regress affected work. Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver). Protect OHP, pull-ups and shoulder work. Stop/regress throws if they impair OHP; a missing rotation/power exposure is recorded honestly. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


### Week 6 — Friday

Squat-centered lower session; moderate dips and pull-ups; Copenhagen. Impact occurs first.

**Impact clock: 30 minutes maximum including travel and preparation.** Planned activity at the site: 8:29; full dose leaves 21:31 for travel/extra preparation.

| Order | Activity | Dose / full recovery | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Low bilateral pogos | 1×10; 30 s between sets | 10 |
| 3 | Gentle pre-run adductor squeeze | 3×20 s, 20 s between; ~20–30% effort, not a clearance test | 100 |
| 4 | Running setup | Check measured route, clear runout and gradual stopping; no extra accelerations | 30 |
| 5 | Accelerations with equal-distance runout | 2×15 m + 15 m easy runout each, ~5% hill, 60% perceived effort; 120 s after completing runout before next rep | 144 |
| 6 | Ordinary transitions / measuring | Marker changes, measurement and movement between drills not already counted | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 10, moderate 0, high 0. **Running:** 30 acceleration + 30 runout = 60 m.

Start the clock before travel. Compare actual transit/setup plus this full prescription with the cap BEFORE work. Cut last run (normally 132 s saved), then final high training jump (100 s), then another run; Wednesday cut the final high training jump or final moderate set as applicable. Preserve full preparation/rest. A test needs all three planned attempts for the best-of-three protocol; if it cannot fit, defer the measurement rather than call a partial test complete. Record actual contacts/meters; reduced work does not qualify a larger stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday.

Progress only after two actually tolerated exposures of the same relevant mode/stage and normal next mornings. Running exposures and jump exposures are separate. Deload technique does not qualify a higher stage; restore only a dose actually tolerated before deload. Outside approved test, first-entry and post-deload restoration exceptions, compare actual weekly contacts by tier with the last completed comparable normal week: keep any increase ≤15% (round down to whole contacts); small high-tier totals usually mean hold. Time/symptom cuts do not authorize a larger automatic next-week dose. Review any proposed upward exception. No faster run stage is added because Friday has more time.

After impact and the following morning record normal/abnormal. If abnormal: location, severity, onset/duration, gait/stride/squat effect and gentle resisted-adduction response. Familiar mild soreness resolving in 24–48 h without movement change: hold, do not progress. Increasing/persistent symptoms or reduced output: regress affected work. Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

**Projected strength total: 52:30 (52.5 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Double-kettlebell clean fallback:** Familiar two-hand swing, same sets of 5 with 90 s rest; otherwise light two-hand KB deadlift, same sets of 3 with fast stand/controlled return. Do not learn a new fallback in W6.
**Supported dumbbell single-leg RDL fallback:** Same single-leg hinge with lighter load/shorter ROM and hand support; no substitution to the excluded bilateral variation
**Prone Y fallback:** Familiar bilateral prone Y/T 2×12, same easy reserve and 60 s rest; same two-arm timing allowance. An unplanned unilateral replacement needs a new clock.
**Landmine rotation fallback:** High-to-low cable chop, identical sets/reps/reserve/rest. Distinct from Wednesday’s low-to-high lift fallback. Avoid forced lumbar twisting; choose the familiar version before reduced weeks.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If recovery binds: added single-leg hinge first, then reduce nonessential remaining work. More available time is for full preparation/recovery, not bonus sets. Protect safe tests/main lifts and shoulder/adductor work; symptoms override any cut label. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

**Projected strength total: 68:00 (68 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Single-arm kettlebell clean fallback:** Independent-arm chest-supported machine speed row, same sets, 3/side, ≤50% of that day’s chosen normal row load, fast pull/2 s return; same rest. Upper-body alternative, not an equivalent ballistic stimulus.
**Chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. If no independent-arm station exists Wednesday: use bilateral chest-supported rows at the same set count AND replace its suitcase carry with one-arm overhead KB hold 1×15 s/side, easy, no press reps. This separate upper-limb hold supplies unilateral work and costs ≤the carry allowance; flag the lost carry exposure, a flexible floor.
**Cable external rotation fallback:** Band external rotation 2×12/side, same reserve/rest/side-change allowance.
**Ab wheel fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver) → secondary bench. Protect dip and shoulder work. **Projected strength total: 73:00 (73 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Two-hand kettlebell swing fallback:** Light two-hand KB deadlift, same sets, 3 reps, fast stand and controlled return without release/jump; 90 s rest. Technique/fast-intent fallback, not a ballistic equivalent.
**Reverse lunge fallback:** Assisted reverse lunge with reduced ROM/load
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If local fatigue or schedule binds: added lunge dose first, then a Pallof set; do not relabel Copenhagen as the additional accessory. Preserve safe pull-up, conventional and second OHP exposure. If protected work still cannot fit the real window, record the missed work and revise rather than rush rests. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.

Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.

**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If recovery binds: added single-leg hinge first, then reduce nonessential remaining work. More available time is for full preparation/recovery, not bonus sets. Protect safe tests/main lifts and shoulder/adductor work; symptoms override any cut label. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


## Week 8

Fresh dip priority; subordinate paused bench; horizontal pulling. Start ≥6 h after the long ride FINISHES.

**Before strength:** no impact. Sunday starts ≥6 hours after actual ride completion; no impact after a ride or fatigued lifting.

**Projected strength total: 67:30 (67.5 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Single-arm kettlebell clean fallback:** Independent-arm chest-supported machine speed row, same sets, 3/side, ≤50% of that day’s chosen normal row load, fast pull/2 s return; same rest. Upper-body alternative, not an equivalent ballistic stimulus.
**Chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. If no independent-arm station exists Wednesday: use bilateral chest-supported rows at the same set count AND replace its suitcase carry with one-arm overhead KB hold 1×15 s/side, easy, no press reps. This separate upper-limb hold supplies unilateral work and costs ≤the carry allowance; flag the lost carry exposure, a flexible floor.
**Cable external rotation fallback:** Band external rotation 2×12/side, same reserve/rest/side-change allowance.
**Ab wheel fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver) → secondary bench. Protect dip and shoulder work. Sunday power must stay easy on the legs after the long ride; regress to the supported upper-body fallback if needed. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.

### Week 8 — Wednesday

Fresh heavy OHP priority; pull-ups, rows and protected shoulder/trunk work.

**Impact clock: 15 minutes maximum including travel and preparation.** Planned activity at the site: 13:55; full dose leaves 1:05 for travel/extra preparation.

| Order | Activity | Dose / full recovery | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Low bilateral pogos | 4×10; 30 s between sets | 130 |
| 3 | Broad-jump rehearsals | One at 50% and one at 70%; 60 s between; reset included | 80 |
| 4 | Standing broad jump training | 4 single attempts; 90 s after last rehearsal and between attempts | 400 |
| 5 | Ordinary transitions / measuring | Marker changes, measurement and movement between drills not already counted | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 40, moderate 2, high 4. **Running:** 0 acceleration + 0 runout = 0 m.

Start the clock before travel. Compare actual transit/setup plus this full prescription with the cap BEFORE work. Cut last run (normally 132 s saved), then final high training jump (100 s), then another run; Wednesday cut the final high training jump or final moderate set as applicable. Preserve full preparation/rest. A test needs all three planned attempts for the best-of-three protocol; if it cannot fit, defer the measurement rather than call a partial test complete. Record actual contacts/meters; reduced work does not qualify a larger stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday.

Progress only after two actually tolerated exposures of the same relevant mode/stage and normal next mornings. Running exposures and jump exposures are separate. Deload technique does not qualify a higher stage; restore only a dose actually tolerated before deload. Outside approved test, first-entry and post-deload restoration exceptions, compare actual weekly contacts by tier with the last completed comparable normal week: keep any increase ≤15% (round down to whole contacts); small high-tier totals usually mean hold. Time/symptom cuts do not authorize a larger automatic next-week dose. Review any proposed upward exception. No faster run stage is added because Friday has more time.

After impact and the following morning record normal/abnormal. If abnormal: location, severity, onset/duration, gait/stride/squat effect and gentle resisted-adduction response. Familiar mild soreness resolving in 24–48 h without movement change: hold, do not progress. Increasing/persistent symptoms or reduced output: regress affected work. Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

**Projected strength total: 71:30 (71.5 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw fallback:** Low-to-high cable lift: same sets/reps per side, lightest smooth setting, fast outward phase/controlled return; same side changes and 90 s rests. This preserves a distinct dynamic pattern but is not a ballistic throw.
**Single-arm chest-supported machine row fallback:** Chest-supported independent-arm lever/T-bar machine for unilateral work; bilateral chest-supported machine/T-bar/seal row for Sunday. If no independent-arm station exists Wednesday: use bilateral chest-supported rows at the same set count AND replace its suitcase carry with one-arm overhead KB hold 1×15 s/side, easy, no press reps. This separate upper-limb hold supplies unilateral work and costs ≤the carry allowance; flag the lost carry exposure, a flexible floor.
**Face pull fallback:** Familiar bilateral prone Y/T 2×12, same easy reserve and 60 s rest; same two-arm timing allowance. An unplanned unilateral replacement needs a new clock.
**Hanging leg raise fallback:** Bent-knee hanging raise if straight-leg raises swing; for body saw use safe sliders/towel on a suitable surface, otherwise the already familiar short-range ab wheel; hollow hold may use a tucked lever. No uncontrolled repetitions.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver). Protect OHP, pull-ups and shoulder work. Stop/regress throws if they impair OHP; a missing rotation/power exposure is recorded honestly. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


### Week 8 — Friday

Squat-centered lower session; moderate dips and pull-ups; Copenhagen. Impact occurs first.

**Impact clock: 30 minutes maximum including travel and preparation.** Planned activity at the site: 16:01; full dose leaves 13:59 for travel/extra preparation.

| Order | Activity | Dose / full recovery | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Low bilateral pogos | 2×10; 30 s between sets | 50 |
| 3 | Broad-jump rehearsals | One at 50% and one at 70%; 60 s between; reset included | 80 |
| 4 | Standing broad jump training | 2 single attempts; 90 s after last rehearsal and between attempts | 200 |
| 5 | Gentle pre-run adductor squeeze | 3×20 s, 20 s between; ~20–30% effort, not a clearance test | 100 |
| 6 | Running setup | Check measured route, clear runout and gradual stopping; no extra accelerations | 30 |
| 7 | Accelerations with equal-distance runout | 3×20 m + 20 m easy runout each, ~5% hill, 70% perceived effort; 120 s after completing runout before next rep | 276 |
| 8 | Ordinary transitions / measuring | Marker changes, measurement and movement between drills not already counted | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 20, moderate 2, high 2. **Running:** 60 acceleration + 60 runout = 120 m.

Start the clock before travel. Compare actual transit/setup plus this full prescription with the cap BEFORE work. Cut last run (normally 132 s saved), then final high training jump (100 s), then another run; Wednesday cut the final high training jump or final moderate set as applicable. Preserve full preparation/rest. A test needs all three planned attempts for the best-of-three protocol; if it cannot fit, defer the measurement rather than call a partial test complete. Record actual contacts/meters; reduced work does not qualify a larger stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday.

Progress only after two actually tolerated exposures of the same relevant mode/stage and normal next mornings. Running exposures and jump exposures are separate. Deload technique does not qualify a higher stage; restore only a dose actually tolerated before deload. Outside approved test, first-entry and post-deload restoration exceptions, compare actual weekly contacts by tier with the last completed comparable normal week: keep any increase ≤15% (round down to whole contacts); small high-tier totals usually mean hold. Time/symptom cuts do not authorize a larger automatic next-week dose. Review any proposed upward exception. No faster run stage is added because Friday has more time.

After impact and the following morning record normal/abnormal. If abnormal: location, severity, onset/duration, gait/stride/squat effect and gentle resisted-adduction response. Familiar mild soreness resolving in 24–48 h without movement change: hold, do not progress. Increasing/persistent symptoms or reduced output: regress affected work. Acute sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms OR movement-altering discomfort: suspend affected impact and seek clinical evaluation. No aggressive static adductor stretching before running.

**Standing broad jump — daily power fallback:** If high work is not ready, only already tolerated low-tier pogos from the impact card may serve as a reduced power exposure. No added contacts; absent impact means power is omitted and logged.
**Supported dumbbell single-leg RDL fallback:** Same single-leg hinge with lighter load/shorter ROM and hand support; no substitution to the excluded bilateral variation
**Prone Y fallback:** Familiar bilateral prone Y/T 2×12, same easy reserve and 60 s rest; same two-arm timing allowance. An unplanned unilateral replacement needs a new clock.
**Landmine rotation fallback:** High-to-low cable chop, identical sets/reps/reserve/rest. Distinct from Wednesday’s low-to-high lift fallback. Avoid forced lumbar twisting; choose the familiar version before reduced weeks.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If recovery binds: added single-leg hinge first, then reduce nonessential remaining work. More available time is for full preparation/recovery, not bonus sets. Protect safe tests/main lifts and shoulder/adductor work; symptoms override any cut label. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

Fresh dip priority; subordinate paused bench; horizontal pulling. Start ≥6 h after the long ride FINISHES.

**Biceps progression:** Normal curls: 2×8–12 at 3 RIR, 75 s between sets. First prescribed set selects a light working load; no extra failure test. On the next NORMAL appearance of that same variation add reps while preserving ROM/reserve. After BOTH sets reach 12, increase the smallest suitable load next normal appearance and return toward 8. Compare only the same implement/setting; deload appearances neither qualify an increase nor reset the record. W6/W12: 1×8 at ≥5 RIR with reduced load. Alternating curls count 8–12 per arm.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If time or recovery binds: final biceps set → carry → a row set (row-floor waiver) → secondary bench. Protect dip and shoulder work. Sunday power must stay easy on the legs after the long ride; regress to the supported upper-body fallback if needed. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

**Projected strength total: 73:00 (73 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Two-hand kettlebell swing fallback:** Light two-hand KB deadlift, same sets, 3 reps, fast stand and controlled return without release/jump; 90 s rest. Technique/fast-intent fallback, not a ballistic equivalent.
**Reverse lunge fallback:** Assisted reverse lunge with reduced ROM/load
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If local fatigue or schedule binds: added lunge dose first, then a Pallof set; do not relabel Copenhagen as the additional accessory. Preserve safe pull-up, conventional and second OHP exposure. If protected work still cannot fit the real window, record the missed work and revise rather than rush rests. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.

**Projected strength total: 68:00 (68 minutes), including delay reserve.** The 75-minute suggestion is not a hard stop; preserve recovery and log actual time. Friday has additional availability. No duration here is measured.
**Standing broad jump — daily power fallback:** If high work is not ready, only already tolerated low-tier pogos from the impact card may serve as a reduced power exposure. No added contacts; absent impact means power is omitted and logged.
**Supported dumbbell single-leg RDL fallback:** Same single-leg hinge with lighter load/shorter ROM and hand support; no substitution to the excluded bilateral variation
**Prone Y fallback:** Familiar bilateral prone Y/T 2×12, same easy reserve and 60 s rest; same two-arm timing allowance. An unplanned unilateral replacement needs a new clock.
**Landmine rotation fallback:** High-to-low cable chop, identical sets/reps/reserve/rest. Distinct from Wednesday’s low-to-high lift fallback. Avoid forced lumbar twisting; choose the familiar version before reduced weeks.
**Load rules:** All main-lift numbers are conditional nominal loads. If a first set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, DL −10 lb. If ≥2 RIR harder, double that reduction and remove an affected final back-off if needed. No grinders, missed-increment catch-up or forced depth. Progress only with comparable successful technique/reserve and normal tissue response. Log bodyweight plus external load for dips/pull-ups; examples use BW 170, not a measured current weight.
**Power rules:** Power is fast, forceful practice with low fatigue, not a circuit. Select a load permitting crisp repeatable reps; stop at visible slowing, unstable rack/landing, arm-dominant clean, local fatigue, or deterioration of the next main-lift ramp. Use 90 s between normal sets/pairs; extend to 120 s or longer if required, with time logged. After TWO comparable crisp exposures and normal next-day response, increase only the smallest suitable load step at the next normal occurrence. Hold when that increment is too large. Do not raise power load and leg-accessory load or impact stage in the same week. No added sets are needed in this baseline. W6/W12 reduce dose and load/effort, using a familiar version. A stopped movement is logged as omitted; no checklist overrides symptoms.
**Leg progression:** Additional leg work: one paired work set at 4 RIR, with support/ROM that preserves control. Begin with the printed unloaded rehearsal; choose a light load using the first prescribed work pair, not an extra capacity test. After two comparable normal exposures with ≥4 RIR, normal next-day response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts, increase the smallest load step only. Otherwise hold; reduce load/ROM or increase balance support if needed. Do not increase this load in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 1×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter and familiar. Copenhagen remains separate and mandatory when tolerated.
**Core progression:** Keep the printed core variation for its three-week block. Start with a short controlled range; after two comparable successful exposures improve ROM, or the smallest load step for cable/landmine work, without adding sets or changing a second variable. No swinging on hanging raises; use bent knees if necessary. Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good-position capacity left. W6/W12 use the reduced familiar dose and ≥5 RIR/large position reserve. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation.
**Cuts and consequences:** If recovery binds: added single-leg hinge first, then reduce nonessential remaining work. More available time is for full preparation/recovery, not bonus sets. Protect safe tests/main lifts and shoulder/adductor work; symptoms override any cut label. One lost pulling set gives 18/14=1.286, but a row cut breaks the six-row floor. Two lost pulling sets give 18/13=1.385: prospectively reduce secondary pressing to ≤16, giving 16/13=1.231. If pressing is already completed, report the actual missed ratio; never add fatigued pulling or rewrite history. Saturday stays rest.
**Log:** actual sets/reps/load and system load; reserve/technique; crisp/normal/grindy final priority set; power load and quality; actual strength/transit/impact time; contacts/runout; next-morning tissue response; prescribed rides completed. First two weeks remain normal training while establishing actual timing and accessory tolerance. Printed holds/regressions are authorized contingencies; unplanned primary replacement/frequency changes or upward impact/target changes require review.


## Week 10

### Week 10 — Sunday

Fresh dip priority; subordinate paused bench; horizontal pulling. Start ≥6 h after the long ride FINISHES.

**Before strength:** no impact. Sunday starts ≥6 hours after actual ride completion; no impact after a ride or fatigued lifting.
